<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the abelian Zilber–Pink conjecture over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$. Every irreducible subvariety of an abelian variety has only finitely many maximal atypical subvarieties, where atypicality is measured inside its smallest containing torsion coset.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every number field $K$, every abelian variety $A/K$, and every irreducible subvariety $X\subseteq A_{\bar K}$, there are only finitely many maximal atypical subvarieties of $X$ in $S=\langle X\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$. Equivalently, there are finitely many proper atypical subvarieties $Y_1,\ldots,Y_m\subsetneq X$ such that every atypical subvariety is contained in one of the $Y_j$.
  - Explanation: Finiteness of maximal atypical subvarieties is measured within the smallest containing torsion coset.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-nondensity)): Let $A$ be an abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and let $X\subseteq A$ be an irreducible subvariety contained in no proper torsion coset. Then $X\cap A^{[\dim X+1]}$ is not Zariski dense in $X$.
  - Explanation: High-codimension torsion-coset intersections cannot be Zariski dense under the stated noncontainment hypothesis.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-rank-translates)): Let $A$ be an abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, let $\Gamma\subseteq A(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ be a subgroup satisfying $$\dim_{\mathbb Q}\bigl(\Gamma\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q\bigr)<\infty,$$ and let $X\subseteq A$ be an irreducible closed subvariety of dimension $d$ contained in no translate of a proper algebraic subgroup of $A$. Then $$X(\overline{\mathbb Q})\cap\bigl(A^{[d+1]}(\overline{\mathbb Q})+\Gamma\bigr)$$ is not Zariski dense in $X$. If $d=1$, this intersection is finite.
  - Explanation: Finite-rank subgroup translates preserve non-density under the stronger exclusion of proper subgroup translates; the curve intersection is finite.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-real-calculus)): Let $G,G'$ be abelian varieties over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with the preceding choices. Every element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G,G')_{\mathbb R}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G,G')\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R$ acts on both $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$ and $\mathcal H(G)$. The Lie action is faithful. Convergence in this finite-dimensional real homomorphism space implies operator-norm convergence on the height spaces.* *The polarization adjoint of a real homomorphism is a real homomorphism. Its kernel and image in the Lie algebras have orthogonal projectors in the corresponding real endomorphism algebras, and it has a generalized inverse in the opposite real homomorphism space. In particular, if $f$ is fixed and $\\|f(x_i)\\|\to0$, then $$\\|P_{(\ker f)^\perp}(x_i)\\|\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Real homomorphisms link Lie-algebra projectors to bounded operators on canonical height spaces.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-sequence)): For sequence data as in Definition <a href="#def:sequence-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:sequence-data">5</a>, let $V\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(W)$ be the image of an idempotent in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\mathcal A)_{\mathbb R}$. Let $\Phi$ be the orthogonal projector with kernel $V$, and put $$q_i=(y_{i\ell}/\sqrt{H_{i\ell}})_{\ell\in I} \in\mathcal H(\mathcal A).$$ The following two conditions cannot both hold: $$\label{eq:forbidden-sequence} \mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(W)}V>\sum_{\ell\in I}m_\ell, \qquad \\|\Phi(q_i)\\|\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The strict codimension condition and vanishing normalized projection are incompatible for sequence data.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-height-decomposition)): Let $A$ be a nonzero abelian variety and $x_i\in A(\overline{\mathbb Q})$. After passing to a subsequence, there is an isogeny $$\alpha:A\longrightarrow\prod_{\ell\in I}W_\ell$$ with nonzero factors, and scales $H_{i\ell}\ge1$, such that the coordinates $p_{i\ell}$ of $\alpha(x_i)$ have norms $O(\sqrt{H_{i\ell}})$ and successive scale ratios tend to zero. There is at most one bounded scale, placed first and equal to one. Every other scale tends to infinity and satisfies <a href="#eq:sequence-nondegenerate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sequence-nondegenerate">[eq:sequence-nondegenerate]</a> with $A_\ell=W_\ell$ for every fixed nonzero homomorphism on $W_\ell$.
  - Explanation: An isogeny and subsequence separate bounded and divergent height scales with nonvanishing quotient images.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sequence-implies-nondensity)): The auxiliary sequence theorem implies Theorem <a href="#thm:nondensity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:nondensity">2</a> for every abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary sequence theorem supplies universal non-density.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-projection)): Suppose that sequence data as in Definition <a href="#def:sequence-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:sequence-data">5</a>, together with $V$ and $\Phi$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:sequence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:sequence">6</a>, satisfy <a href="#eq:forbidden-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:forbidden-sequence">[eq:forbidden-sequence]</a> and have least $\dim\mathcal A$ among counterexamples with the same presence or absence of a low factor. If a low factor occurs, assume also that Theorem <a href="#thm:sequence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:sequence">6</a> has already been proved for all-high data in every dimension. There do not exist fixed abelian subvarieties $C_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$, with $C=\prod_\ell C_\ell\ne0$, and nonnegative integers $a_\ell$ such that, on an infinite subsequence, $$\label{eq:projection-obstruction} \dim_{y_{i\ell}}\bigl(Y_\ell\cap(y_{i\ell}+C_\ell)\bigr) \ge a_\ell\quad(\ell\in I), \qquad \sum_\ell a_\ell\ge\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(\Phi\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(C)}).$$
  - Explanation: Minimal ambient counterexamples exclude product quotients whose fiber dimensions compensate for lost projector rank.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ax-product)): Let $A_\ell$, $\ell\in I$, be complex abelian varieties, let $Z_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$ be subvarieties, and put $Z=\prod_{\ell\in I}Z_\ell$ and $A=\prod_{\ell\in I}A_\ell$. Let $g\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(A)\longrightarrow E$ be a complex linear map to a Hermitian vector space. If $P_Z(g)=0$, there are abelian subvarieties $C_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$ such that, for every $z=(z_\ell)_\ell\in Z$, $$\label{eq:ax-product-obstruction} \sum_{\ell\in I} \dim_{z_\ell}\bigl(Z_\ell\cap(z_\ell+C_\ell)\bigr) >\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\left(g\bigm\|_{\bigoplus_{\ell\in I}\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\ell)}\right).$$ In particular, $\prod_\ell C_\ell$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: Failure of tangent injectivity produces a nonzero product subgroup with a strict fiber-dimension surplus.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-directions)): Fix subvarieties $Y_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$ of complex abelian varieties. For each $\ell$, there is a finite set $\mathcal H_\ell$ of abelian subvarieties of $A_\ell$ with the following property. Suppose that $y_\ell\in Z_\ell\subseteq Y_\ell$, that $C_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$ are abelian subvarieties, and that $g\colon\bigoplus_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(A_\ell)\longrightarrow E$ is complex linear. Put $$d_\ell=\dim_{y_\ell}\bigl(Z_\ell\cap(y_\ell+C_\ell)\bigr).$$ If $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}$ and $$\sum_\ell d_\ell\geq \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\left(g\bigm\|_{\bigoplus_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\ell)}\right) +\varepsilon,$$ there are $H_\ell\in\mathcal H_\ell$ such that $$\label{eq:fixed-parent-rank} \sum_\ell\dim_{y_\ell} \bigl(Y_\ell\cap(y_\ell+H_\ell)\bigr) \geq\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\left(g\bigm\|_{\bigoplus_\ell\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(H_\ell)}\right) +\varepsilon.$$ If $\sum_\ell d_\ell>0$, the product $\prod_\ell H_\ell$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: Fixed parent varieties admit finite sets of subgroup directions preserving the stated local rank comparisons.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tangent)): Consider fixed sequence data as in Definition <a href="#def:sequence-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:sequence-data">5</a>, and let $V$ and $\Phi$ be as in Theorem <a href="#thm:sequence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:sequence">6</a>. Suppose that the conclusion of Lemma <a href="#lemma:projection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:projection">9</a> excludes every fixed nonzero product $C=\prod_\ell C_\ell$ for which $$\label{eq:excluded-tangent-configuration} \sum_\ell\dim_{y_{i\ell}} \bigl(Y_\ell\cap(y_{i\ell}+C_\ell)\bigr) \geq\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\bigl(\Phi\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(C)}\bigr)$$ holds for infinitely many $i$. Put $r=\dim\mathcal A+1$, and, for a linear map $g$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathcal A)$, define $$\Delta g(v_1,\ldots,v_r) =\bigl(g(v_2)-g(v_1),\ldots,g(v_r)-g(v_{r-1})\bigr).$$ Then the following assertions hold.* 1. *There are a neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $\Phi$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\mathcal A)_{\mathbb R}$ and an index $i_0$ such that $P_Z(g)>0$ for every $i\geq i_0$, every $g\in\mathcal U$, and every subproduct $Z=\prod_\ell Z_\ell$ with $y_{i\ell}\in Z_\ell\subseteq Y_\ell$.* 2. *For every sufficiently large $i$ and every such subproduct $Z$, $P_{Z^r}(\Delta\Phi)>0$.
  - Explanation: Projection exclusion yields tangent positivity near the limiting projector and for consecutive differences.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-intersection-lower-bound)): Let $\mathcal B$ be a fixed closed box with rational endpoints in the coordinates of an integral basis of $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\mathcal A)$. There are positive integers $\theta,\omega_1$, depending only on the fixed ambients, polarizations, and box, with the following property. For every irreducible subproduct $Z\subseteq\mathcal A$ such that $P_Z$ is strictly positive everywhere on $\mathcal B$, $$\label{eq:intersection-lower-bound} P_Z(g)\ge \theta^{-1}D_Z^{-\omega_1} \qquad (g\in\mathcal B).$$
  - Explanation: Strict positivity on a fixed rational box yields a degree-dependent lower bound with constants depending on fixed geometric data.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-dill-form)): Let $X_1,\ldots,X_s$ be fixed irreducible positive-dimensional projective varieties over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, with $s\ge2$, and fix embeddings $X_\ell\subseteq\mathbb P^{N_\ell}$ given by very ample bundles $L_\ell$. For positive integral weights $a_\ell$, put $N_a=\boxtimes_\ell L_\ell^{a_\ell}$. Let $\mathcal M$ be a nef bundle on $\prod_\ell X_\ell$ and $\mathcal P=N_a^{t_1}$, with its full monomial coordinate system $\Xi$. Suppose $\mathcal P\otimes\mathcal M^{-1}$ is generated by $J$ sections $\mathcal Z$, whose images under an injection $$\mathcal P\otimes\mathcal M^{-1}\hookrightarrow N_a^{t_2}$$ are polynomials of multidegree $t_2a$ with joint coefficient height at most $\sum_\ell a_\ell\delta_\ell$. Assume the injection is an isomorphism on a nonempty open set $U^0$. Here $t_1,t_2,J$ are positive integers and $\delta_\ell\ge1$.* *Let $x=(x_\ell)\in U^0(\overline{\mathbb Q})$. Suppose that, for every irreducible subproduct $Z=\prod_\ell Z_\ell$ with $x_\ell\in Z_\ell\subseteq X_\ell$, $$\label{eq:dill-intersections} (\mathcal M^{\dim Z}\cdot Z) \ge \theta^{-1}\prod_\ell (\deg Z_\ell)^{-\omega}a_\ell^{\dim Z_\ell},$$ where $\theta\ge1$ and $\omega\ge-1$ are integers. Put $$\begin{split} u_0&=\sum_\ell\dim X_\ell,\\ \Lambda&=\theta(2t_1u_0)^{u_0} \max_\ell(N_\ell+1)\prod_\ell\deg X_\ell,\\ \psi(u)&=\prod_{j=u+1}^{u_0}((3+\omega)j+1), \qquad c_1=c_2=\Lambda^{\psi(0)},\\ c_3^{(\ell)}&=\Lambda^{2\psi(0)}(Jt_2)^{u_0} (h(X_\ell)+\delta_\ell). \end{split}$$ If $a_\ell\ge c_2a_{\ell+1}$ for $\ell<s$ and $h(x_\ell)\ge c_3^{(\ell)}$ for every $\ell$, then $$\label{eq:dill-height} \sum_\ell a_\ell h(x_\ell) \le c_1\bigl(h(\Xi(x))-h(\mathcal Z(x))\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Dill’s inequality controls weighted projective heights under intersection, weight-separation and height-threshold hypotheses.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-all-high)): Theorem <a href="#thm:sequence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:sequence">6</a> holds for all-high sequence data.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary sequence theorem holds when every scale diverges.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-height-kernel)): Fix integers $N,D\geq 1$. Let $F$ be a number field and let $\mathcal S$ be a set of points in $\mathbb P^N(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ of height at most $T$, where $T\geq1$. Suppose the vector space of homogeneous degree-$D$ polynomials vanishing on $\mathcal S$ is defined over $F$. This space has an $F$-basis whose coefficient heights are $O_{N,D}(T)$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing polynomial spaces admit bounded-height bases over their field of definition without bounding the degrees of evaluation points.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-bounded-conormal)): Let $Z\subseteq\mathbb P^N$ be an irreducible projective variety of degree $D$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. At every smooth point of $Z$, the differentials of homogeneous equations of $Z$ of degree at most $D$, dehomogenized in an affine chart, span its conormal space.
  - Explanation: Equations of degree bounded by the variety degree generate conormal directions at smooth points.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-minimal-products)): For a minimal admissible choice, the following assertions hold after discarding finitely many indices and passing to a subsequence.* 1. *For every pair of positive integers $D,B$, there is an index $i_0(D,B)$ such that, for every $i\ge i_0(D,B)$ and every factor $\ell$, any hypersurface of degree at most $D$ defined over an extension $F_i/k_i$ with $[F_i:k_i]\le B$ which contains $y_{i\ell}$ also contains $Z_{i\ell}$.* 2. *For each fixed degree, the equations of $Z_{i\ell}$ in that degree have a $k_i$-basis of coefficient heights $O(H_{i\ell})$. The points $y_{i\ell}$ are smooth on $Z_{i\ell}$, and equations of bounded degree and height $O(H_{i\ell})$ span their conormal spaces.* 3. *The low factor belongs to a finite set of varieties. We may therefore suppose $Z_{i0}=Z_0$ independent of $i$.
  - Explanation: Minimal admissible products exclude proper bounded-degree cuts over bounded extensions and provide smooth marked points and a fixed low factor.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-fixed-low-point)): In a minimal admissible product for the putative low-factor counterexample, $d_0>0$.
  - Explanation: The low factor of a minimal admissible counterexample must have positive dimension.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-arithmetic-positive)): Let $Z_i=\prod_{\ell=0}^s Z_{i\ell}$ be the bounded-degree, bounded-field-degree subproducts above, with fixed positive-dimensional low factor $Z_0$, dense factorwise height bounds $h_{L_\ell}=O(H_{i\ell})$, and fixed high cycle classes. Suppose the two tangent integrals for $\Phi$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:tangent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tangent">12</a> are positive. For the homomorphisms and scalings in <a href="#eq:scaled-homomorphisms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:scaled-homomorphisms">[eq:scaled-homomorphisms]</a>, one has $$\label{eq:arithmetic-positive} \liminf_{i\to\infty} \frac{(\bar E_i^{d+1}\cdot Z_i)}{M_i^2B_i}>0.$$
  - Explanation: Distinct limiting curvature measures force positivity of the normalized arithmetic intersection quotient.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-small-sections)): For the products $Z_i$, bundles $\bar E_i$, and scalings above, there is a constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $i$, a projective resolution $\pi_i:\widetilde Z_i\to Z_i$ over $k_i$ carries a nonzero section defined over $k_i$, $$s_i\in H^0(\widetilde Z_i,\pi_i^*E_i^{\otimes n_i}) \quad\text{for some integer }n_i>0$$ whose adelic supremum norms satisfy $$\label{eq:small-section-norms} \\|s_i\\|_{v,\mathrm{sup}}\le \begin{cases} \exp(-cn_iM_i^2),&v\mid\infty,\\ 1,&v\nmid\infty. \end{cases}$$ The resolution may be chosen to be an isomorphism over the smooth locus.
  - Explanation: Arithmetic positivity yields nonzero sections with exponential archimedean norm decay over the original bounded-degree fields.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index)): Fix projective embeddings of abelian varieties $A_\ell$ by symmetric very ample bundles $L_\ell$, a symmetric very ample canonically metrized bundle $\bar L$ on $\mathcal A=\prod_\ell A_\ell$, and integral bases of the homomorphism groups between the fixed varieties. Let $F_i:\mathcal A\to\mathcal A$ be homomorphisms. Suppose that:* 1. *$y_{i\ell}$ is a smooth point of an irreducible $Z_{i\ell}\subset A_\ell$, its projective height is $O(H_{i\ell})$, and its conormal space is spanned by equations of bounded degree and coefficient height $O(H_{i\ell})$, with $H_{i\ell}\ge1$;* 2. *$M_i\longrightarrow\infty$, the positive integers $b_{i\ell}$ satisfy $b_{i\ell}\ll M_i$ and $b_{i\ell}^2H_{i\ell}\asymp M_i^2$, and the coefficients of $F_i\|_{A_\ell}$ in the fixed bases are $O(b_{i\ell})$;* 3. *for $\bar E_i=F_i^*\bar L$ one has $h_{\bar E_i}(y_i)=o(M_i^2)$, and a nonzero section $s_i$ of $E_i^{n_i}$ on a resolution of $Z_i=\prod_\ell Z_{i\ell}$ satisfies $$\label{eq:index-small-section} \\|s_i\\|_{v,\sup}\le \begin{cases} e^{-c n_iM_i^2},&v\mid\infty,\\ 1,&v\nmid\infty, \end{cases}$$ for a fixed $c>0$, over a number field containing its data.* *Assume that the resolution is an isomorphism over the smooth locus. Then, for some $\eta>0$ independent of $i$, $\operatorname{ind}_{n_i,b_i}(s_i;y_i)\ge\eta$ for all sufficiently large $i$. No bound on the degree of the field of $y_i$ is required.
  - Explanation: Small section norms and uniform local estimates force positive weighted index without bounding marked-point field degrees.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-lemma-conjugate-product-degree)): Suppose that $G$ is defined over a number field $k$, and that a proper geometric component $J=\prod_{\ell=1}^mJ_\ell$ is common to $\mathcal I_\sigma$ and $\mathcal I_{\sigma+\epsilon}$, where $\sigma>0$ and $0<\epsilon\le1$, and neither level is a rational derivative weight. If $q$ is the number of conjugates of $J$ over $k$, then $$\label{eq:conjugate-product-degree} q\prod_\ell\deg J_\ell\le C(N,\epsilon).$$ In particular the factors have bounded degrees and are defined over a common extension of $k$ of bounded degree.
  - Explanation: A persistent product component has bounded geometric degrees and bounded field of definition through conjugate counting.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-product-cut)): For the minimal products of Proposition <a href="#prop:minimal-products" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:minimal-products">18</a> with $d_0>0$, the small sections of Lemma <a href="#lemma:small-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lemma:small-sections">21</a> cannot satisfy the positive index conclusion of Proposition <a href="#prop:index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:index">22</a> along an infinite subsequence.
  - Explanation: The product cut contradicts minimality by producing a proper bounded-degree hypersurface over a bounded extension.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-optimal-finite)): Every irreducible subvariety of every abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ contains only finitely many optimal subvarieties.
  - Explanation: Universal non-density implies finiteness of all optimal subvarieties.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-maximal-optimal)): Let $X$ be an irreducible subvariety of an abelian variety and $S=\langle X\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$. Every maximal atypical subvariety of $X$ in $S$ is optimal in $X$.
  - Explanation: Maximal atypical subvarieties are optimal, allowing optimal finiteness to yield the main conclusion.

Let $\\Phi$ be the orthogonal projector with kernel $V$, and put $$q_i=(y_{i\\ell}/\\sqrt{H_{i\\ell}})_{\\ell\\in I}\n \\in\\mathcal H(\\mathcal A).$$ The following two conditions cannot both hold: $$\\label{eq:forbidden-sequence}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{codim}}_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(W)}V>\\sum_{\\ell\\in I}m_\\ell,\n \\qquad \\|\\Phi(q_i)\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C5",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-sequence",
          "exact_quote": "For sequence data as in Definition <a href=\"#def:sequence-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:sequence-data\">5</a>, let $V\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(W)$ be the image of an idempotent in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(\\mathcal A)_{\\mathbb R}$. Let $\\Phi$ be the orthogonal projector with kernel $V$, and put $$q_i=(y_{i\\ell}/\\sqrt{H_{i\\ell}})_{\\ell\\in I}\n \\in\\mathcal H(\\mathcal A).$$ The following two conditions cannot both hold: $$\\label{eq:forbidden-sequence}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{codim}}_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(W)}V>\\sum_{\\ell\\in I}m_\\ell,\n \\qquad \\|\\Phi(q_i)\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-height-decomposition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a nonzero abelian variety and $x_i\\in A(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$. After passing to a subsequence, there is an isogeny $$\\alpha:A\\longrightarrow\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}W_\\ell$$ with nonzero factors, and scales $H_{i\\ell}\\ge1$, such that the coordinates $p_{i\\ell}$ of $\\alpha(x_i)$ have norms $O(\\sqrt{H_{i\\ell}})$ and successive scale ratios tend to zero. There is at most one bounded scale, placed first and equal to one. Every other scale tends to infinity and satisfies <a href=\"#eq:sequence-nondegenerate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sequence-nondegenerate\">[eq:sequence-nondegenerate]</a> with $A_\\ell=W_\\ell$ for every fixed nonzero homomorphism on $W_\\ell$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-height-decomposition",

Every other scale tends to infinity and satisfies <a href=\"#eq:sequence-nondegenerate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sequence-nondegenerate\">[eq:sequence-nondegenerate]</a> with $A_\\ell=W_\\ell$ for every fixed nonzero homomorphism on $W_\\ell$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that sequence data as in Definition <a href=\"#def:sequence-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:sequence-data\">5</a>, together with $V$ and $\\Phi$ as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:sequence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:sequence\">6</a>, satisfy <a href=\"#eq:forbidden-sequence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:forbidden-sequence\">[eq:forbidden-sequence]</a> and have least $\\dim\\mathcal A$ among counterexamples with the same presence or absence of a low factor. If a low factor occurs, assume also that Theorem <a href=\"#thm:sequence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:sequence\">6</a> has already been proved for all-high data in every dimension. There do not exist fixed abelian subvarieties $C_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$, with $C=\\prod_\\ell C_\\ell\\ne0$, and nonnegative integers $a_\\ell$ such that, on an infinite subsequence, $$\\label{eq:projection-obstruction}\n \\dim_{y_{i\\ell}}\\bigl(Y_\\ell\\cap(y_{i\\ell}+C_\\ell)\\bigr)\n \\ge a_\\ell\\quad(\\ell\\in I),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_\\ell a_\\ell\\ge\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}(\\Phi|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C)}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-projection",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that sequence data as in Definition <a href=\"#def:sequence-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:sequence-data\">5</a>, together with $V$ and $\\Phi$ as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:sequence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:sequence\">6</a>, satisfy <a href=\"#eq:forbidden-sequence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:forbidden-sequence\">[eq:forbidden-sequence]</a> and have least $\\dim\\mathcal A$ among counterexamples with the same presence or absence of a low factor.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ax-product",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A_\\ell$, $\\ell\\in I$, be complex abelian varieties, let $Z_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ be subvarieties, and put $Z=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}Z_\\ell$ and $A=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}A_\\ell$. Let $g\\colon\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(A)\\longrightarrow E$ be a complex linear map to a Hermitian vector space. If $P_Z(g)=0$, there are abelian subvarieties $C_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ such that, for every $z=(z_\\ell)_\\ell\\in Z$, $$\\label{eq:ax-product-obstruction}\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in I}\n \\dim_{z_\\ell}\\bigl(Z_\\ell\\cap(z_\\ell+C_\\ell)\\bigr)\n >\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_{\\ell\\in I}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\\ell)}\\right).$$ In particular, $\\prod_\\ell C_\\ell$ is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ax-product",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A_\\ell$, $\\ell\\in I$, be complex abelian varieties, let $Z_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ be subvarieties, and put $Z=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}Z_\\ell$ and $A=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}A_\\ell$. Let $g\\colon\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(A)\\longrightarrow E$ be a complex linear map to a Hermitian vector space. If $P_Z(g)=0$, there are abelian subvarieties $C_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ such that, for every $z=(z_\\ell)_\\ell\\in Z$, $$\\label{eq:ax-product-obstruction}\n \\sum_{\\ell\\in I}\n \\dim_{z_\\ell}\\bigl(Z_\\ell\\cap(z_\\ell+C_\\ell)\\bigr)\n >\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_{\\ell\\in I}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\\ell)}\\right).$$ In particular, $\\prod_\\ell C_\\ell$ is nonzero.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A_\\ell$, $\\ell\\in I$, be complex abelian varieties, let $Z_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ be subvarieties, and put $Z=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}Z_\\ell$ and $A=\\prod_{\\ell\\in I}A_\\ell$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-directions",
        "exact_quote": "Fix subvarieties $Y_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ of complex abelian varieties. For each $\\ell$, there is a finite set $\\mathcal H_\\ell$ of abelian subvarieties of $A_\\ell$ with the following property. Suppose that $y_\\ell\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq Y_\\ell$, that $C_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ are abelian subvarieties, and that $g\\colon\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(A_\\ell)\\longrightarrow E$ is complex linear. Put $$d_\\ell=\\dim_{y_\\ell}\\bigl(Z_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+C_\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}$ and $$\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell\\geq\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon,$$ there are $H_\\ell\\in\\mathcal H_\\ell$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-parent-rank}\n \\sum_\\ell\\dim_{y_\\ell}\n \\bigl(Y_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+H_\\ell)\\bigr)\n \\geq\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(H_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon.$$ If $\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell>0$, the product $\\prod_\\ell H_\\ell$ is nonzero.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-directions",
          "exact_quote": "Fix subvarieties $Y_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ of complex abelian varieties. Put $$d_\\ell=\\dim_{y_\\ell}\\bigl(Z_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+C_\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}$ and $$\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell\\geq\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon,$$ there are $H_\\ell\\in\\mathcal H_\\ell$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-parent-rank}\n \\sum_\\ell\\dim_{y_\\ell}\n \\bigl(Y_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+H_\\ell)\\bigr)\n \\geq\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(H_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon.$$ If $\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell>0$, the product $\\prod_\\ell H_\\ell$ is nonzero.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"

      "exact_statement": "Fix subvarieties $Y_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ of complex abelian varieties.       "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-directions",
          "exact_quote": "Fix subvarieties $Y_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ of complex abelian varieties. For each $\\ell$, there is a finite set $\\mathcal H_\\ell$ of abelian subvarieties of $A_\\ell$ with the following property. Suppose that $y_\\ell\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq Y_\\ell$, that $C_\\ell\\subseteq A_\\ell$ are abelian subvarieties, and that $g\\colon\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(A_\\ell)\\longrightarrow E$ is complex linear. Put $$d_\\ell=\\dim_{y_\\ell}\\bigl(Z_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+C_\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}$ and $$\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell\\geq\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon,$$ there are $H_\\ell\\in\\mathcal H_\\ell$ such that $$\\label{eq:fixed-parent-rank}\n \\sum_\\ell\\dim_{y_\\ell}\n \\bigl(Y_\\ell\\cap(y_\\ell+H_\\ell)\\bigr)\n \\geq\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\left(g\\bigm|_{\\bigoplus_\\ell\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(H_\\ell)}\\right)\n +\\varepsilon.$$ If $\\sum_\\ell d_\\ell>0$, the product $\\prod_\\ell H_\\ell$ is nonzero.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-directions"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tangent",
        "exact_quote": "Consider fixed sequence data as in Definition <a href=\"#def:sequence-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:sequence-data\">5</a>, and let $V$ and $\\Phi$ be as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:sequence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:sequence\">6</a>. Suppose that the conclusion of Lemma <a href=\"#lemma:projection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lemma:projection\">9</a> excludes every fixed nonzero product $C=\\prod_\\ell C_\\ell$ for which $$\\label{eq:excluded-tangent-configuration}\n \\sum_\\ell\\dim_{y_{i\\ell}}\n \\bigl(Y_\\ell\\cap(y_{i\\ell}+C_\\ell)\\bigr)\n \\geq\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\bigl(\\Phi|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(C)}\\bigr)$$ holds for infinitely many $i$. Put $r=\\dim\\mathcal A+1$, and, for a linear map $g$ on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(\\mathcal A)$, define $$\\Delta g(v_1,\\ldots,v_r)\n   =\\bigl(g(v_2)-g(v_1),\\ldots,g(v_r)-g(v_{r-1})\\bigr).$$ Then the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *There are a neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of $\\Phi$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(\\mathcal A)_{\\mathbb R}$ and an index $i_0$ such that $P_Z(g)>0$ for every $i\\geq i_0$, every $g\\in\\mathcal U$, and every subproduct $Z=\\prod_\\ell Z_\\ell$ with $y_{i\\ell}\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq Y_\\ell$.*\n\n2.  *For every sufficiently large $i$ and every such subproduct $Z$, $P_{Z^r}(\\Delta\\Phi)>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tangent",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-intersection-lower-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B$ be a fixed closed box with rational endpoints in the coordinates of an integral basis of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(\\mathcal A)$. There are positive integers $\\theta,\\omega_1$, depending only on the fixed ambients, polarizations, and box, with the following property. For every irreducible subproduct $Z\\subseteq\\mathcal A$ such that $P_Z$ is strictly positive everywhere on $\\mathcal B$, $$\\label{eq:intersection-lower-bound}\n P_Z(g)\\ge \\theta^{-1}D_Z^{-\\omega_1}\n \\qquad (g\\in\\mathcal B).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-intersection-lower-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B$ be a fixed closed box with rational endpoints in the coordinates of an integral basis of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}(\\mathcal A)$.           "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dill-form",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X_1,\\ldots,X_s$ be fixed irreducible positive-dimensional projective varieties over $\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$, with $s\\ge2$, and fix embeddings $X_\\ell\\subseteq\\mathbb P^{N_\\ell}$ given by very ample bundles $L_\\ell$. For positive integral weights $a_\\ell$, put $N_a=\\boxtimes_\\ell L_\\ell^{a_\\ell}$. Let $\\mathcal M$ be a nef bundle on $\\prod_\\ell X_\\ell$ and $\\mathcal P=N_a^{t_1}$, with its full monomial coordinate system $\\Xi$. Suppose $\\mathcal P\\otimes\\mathcal M^{-1}$ is generated by $J$ sections $\\mathcal Z$, whose images under an injection $$\\mathcal P\\otimes\\mathcal M^{-1}\\hookrightarrow N_a^{t_2}$$ are polynomials of multidegree $t_2a$ with joint coefficient height at most $\\sum_\\ell a_\\ell\\delta_\\ell$. Assume the injection is an isomorphism on a nonempty open set $U^0$. Here $t_1,t_2,J$ are positive integers and $\\delta_\\ell\\ge1$.*\n\n\n*Let $x=(x_\\ell)\\in U^0(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$. Suppose that, for every irreducible subproduct $Z=\\prod_\\ell Z_\\ell$ with $x_\\ell\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq X_\\ell$, $$\\label{eq:dill-intersections}\n (\\mathcal M^{\\dim Z}\\cdot Z)\n \\ge \\theta^{-1}\\prod_\\ell\n       (\\deg Z_\\ell)^{-\\omega}a_\\ell^{\\dim Z_\\ell},$$ where $\\theta\\ge1$ and $\\omega\\ge-1$ are integers. Put $$\\begin{split}\n u_0&=\\sum_\\ell\\dim X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\Lambda&=\\theta(2t_1u_0)^{u_0}\n          \\max_\\ell(N_\\ell+1)\\prod_\\ell\\deg X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\psi(u)&=\\prod_{j=u+1}^{u_0}((3+\\omega)j+1),\n \\qquad c_1=c_2=\\Lambda^{\\psi(0)},\\\\\n c_3^{(\\ell)}&=\\Lambda^{2\\psi(0)}(Jt_2)^{u_0}\n                      (h(X_\\ell)+\\delta_\\ell).\n \\end{split}$$ If $a_\\ell\\ge c_2a_{\\ell+1}$ for $\\ell<s$ and $h(x_\\ell)\\ge c_3^{(\\ell)}$ for every $\\ell$, then $$\\label{eq:dill-height}\n \\sum_\\ell a_\\ell h(x_\\ell)\n \\le c_1\\bigl(h(\\Xi(x))-h(\\mathcal Z(x))\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-dill-form",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X_1,\\ldots,X_s$ be fixed irreducible positive-dimensional projective varieties over $\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$, with $s\\ge2$, and fix embeddings $X_\\ell\\subseteq\\mathbb P^{N_\\ell}$ given by very ample bundles $L_\\ell$. For positive integral weights $a_\\ell$, put $N_a=\\boxtimes_\\ell L_\\ell^{a_\\ell}$. Let $\\mathcal M$ be a nef bundle on $\\prod_\\ell X_\\ell$ and $\\mathcal P=N_a^{t_1}$, with its full monomial coordinate system $\\Xi$. Suppose $\\mathcal P\\otimes\\mathcal M^{-1}$ is generated by $J$ sections $\\mathcal Z$, whose images under an injection $$\\mathcal P\\otimes\\mathcal M^{-1}\\hookrightarrow N_a^{t_2}$$ are polynomials of multidegree $t_2a$ with joint coefficient height at most $\\sum_\\ell a_\\ell\\delta_\\ell$. Assume the injection is an isomorphism on a nonempty open set $U^0$. Here $t_1,t_2,J$ are positive integers and $\\delta_\\ell\\ge1$.*\n\n\n*Let $x=(x_\\ell)\\in U^0(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$. Suppose that, for every irreducible subproduct $Z=\\prod_\\ell Z_\\ell$ with $x_\\ell\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq X_\\ell$, $$\\label{eq:dill-intersections}\n (\\mathcal M^{\\dim Z}\\cdot Z)\n \\ge \\theta^{-1}\\prod_\\ell\n       (\\deg Z_\\ell)^{-\\omega}a_\\ell^{\\dim Z_\\ell},$$ where $\\theta\\ge1$ and $\\omega\\ge-1$ are integers. Put $$\\begin{split}\n u_0&=\\sum_\\ell\\dim X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\Lambda&=\\theta(2t_1u_0)^{u_0}\n          \\max_\\ell(N_\\ell+1)\\prod_\\ell\\deg X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\psi(u)&=\\prod_{j=u+1}^{u_0}((3+\\omega)j+1),\n \\qquad c_1=c_2=\\Lambda^{\\psi(0)},\\\\\n c_3^{(\\ell)}&=\\Lambda^{2\\psi(0)}(Jt_2)^{u_0}\n                      (h(X_\\ell)+\\delta_\\ell).\n \\end{split}$$ If $a_\\ell\\ge c_2a_{\\ell+1}$ for $\\ell<s$ and $h(x_\\ell)\\ge c_3^{(\\ell)}$ for every $\\ell$, then $$\\label{eq:dill-height}\n \\sum_\\ell a_\\ell h(x_\\ell)\n \\le c_1\\bigl(h(\\Xi(x))-h(\\mathcal Z(x))\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X_1,\\ldots,X_s$ be fixed irreducible positive-dimensional projective varieties over $\\overline{\\mathbb Q}$, with $s\\ge2$, and fix embeddings $X_\\ell\\subseteq\\mathbb P^{N_\\ell}$ given by very ample bundles $L_\\ell$. Assume the injection is an isomorphism on a nonempty open set $U^0$. Here $t_1,t_2,J$ are positive integers and $\\delta_\\ell\\ge1$.*\n\n\n*Let $x=(x_\\ell)\\in U^0(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$. Put $$\\begin{split}\n u_0&=\\sum_\\ell\\dim X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\Lambda&=\\theta(2t_1u_0)^{u_0}\n          \\max_\\ell(N_\\ell+1)\\prod_\\ell\\deg X_\\ell,\\\\\n \\psi(u)&=\\prod_{j=u+1}^{u_0}((3+\\omega)j+1),\n \\qquad c_1=c_2=\\Lambda^{\\psi(0)},\\\\\n c_3^{(\\ell)}&=\\Lambda^{2\\psi(0)}(Jt_2)^{u_0}\n                      (h(X_\\ell)+\\delta_\\ell).\n \\end{split}$$ If $a_\\ell\\ge c_2a_{\\ell+1}$ for $\\ell<s$ and $h(x_\\ell)\\ge c_3^{(\\ell)}$ for every $\\ell$, then $$\\label{eq:dill-height}\n \\sum_\\ell a_\\ell h(x_\\ell)\n \\le c_1\\bigl(h(\\Xi(x))-h(\\mathcal Z(x))\\bigr).$$",

Suppose that, for every irreducible subproduct $Z=\\prod_\\ell Z_\\ell$ with $x_\\ell\\in Z_\\ell\\subseteq X_\\ell$, $$\\label{eq:dill-intersections}\n (\\mathcal M^{\\dim Z}\\cdot Z)\n \\ge \\theta^{-1}\\prod_\\ell\n       (\\deg Z_\\ell)^{-\\omega}a_\\ell^{\\dim Z_\\ell},$$ where $\\theta\\ge1$ and $\\omega\\ge-1$ are integers.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-minimal-products",
        "exact_quote": "For a minimal admissible choice, the following assertions hold after discarding finitely many indices and passing to a subsequence.*\n\n1.  *For every pair of positive integers $D,B$, there is an index $i_0(D,B)$ such that, for every $i\\ge i_0(D,B)$ and every factor $\\ell$, any hypersurface of degree at most $D$ defined over an extension $F_i/k_i$ with $[F_i:k_i]\\le B$ which contains $y_{i\\ell}$ also contains $Z_{i\\ell}$.*\n\n2.  *For each fixed degree, the equations of $Z_{i\\ell}$ in that degree have a $k_i$-basis of coefficient heights $O(H_{i\\ell})$. The points $y_{i\\ell}$ are smooth on $Z_{i\\ell}$, and equations of bounded degree and height $O(H_{i\\ell})$ span their conormal spaces.*\n\n3.  *The low factor belongs to a finite set of varieties. We may therefore suppose $Z_{i0}=Z_0$ independent of $i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-arithmetic-positive",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z_i=\\prod_{\\ell=0}^s Z_{i\\ell}$ be the bounded-degree, bounded-field-degree subproducts above, with fixed positive-dimensional low factor $Z_0$, dense factorwise height bounds $h_{L_\\ell}=O(H_{i\\ell})$, and fixed high cycle classes. For the homomorphisms and scalings in <a href=\"#eq:scaled-homomorphisms\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:scaled-homomorphisms\">[eq:scaled-homomorphisms]</a>, one has $$\\label{eq:arithmetic-positive}\n \\liminf_{i\\to\\infty}\n \\frac{(\\bar E_i^{d+1}\\cdot Z_i)}{M_i^2B_i}>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-small-sections",
        "exact_quote": "For the products $Z_i$, bundles $\\bar E_i$, and scalings above, there is a constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $i$, a projective resolution $\\pi_i:\\widetilde Z_i\\to Z_i$ over $k_i$ carries a nonzero section defined over $k_i$, $$s_i\\in H^0(\\widetilde Z_i,\\pi_i^*E_i^{\\otimes n_i})\n \\quad\\text{for some integer }n_i>0$$ whose adelic supremum norms satisfy $$\\label{eq:small-section-norms}\n \\|s_i\\|_{v,\\mathrm{sup}}\\le\n \\begin{cases}\n \\exp(-cn_iM_i^2),&v\\mid\\infty,\\\\\n 1,&v\\nmid\\infty.\n \\end{cases}$$ The resolution may be chosen to be an isomorphism over the smooth locus.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-small-sections",
          "exact_quote": "For the products $Z_i$, bundles $\\bar E_i$, and scalings above, there is a constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $i$, a projective resolution $\\pi_i:\\widetilde Z_i\\to Z_i$ over $k_i$ carries a nonzero section defined over $k_i$, $$s_i\\in H^0(\\widetilde Z_i,\\pi_i^*E_i^{\\otimes n_i})\n \\quad\\text{for some integer }n_i>0$$ whose adelic supremum norms satisfy $$\\label{eq:small-section-norms}\n \\|s_i\\|_{v,\\mathrm{sup}}\\le\n \\begin{cases}\n \\exp(-cn_iM_i^2),&v\\mid\\infty,\\\\\n 1,&v\\nmid\\infty.\n \\end{cases}$$ The resolution may be chosen to be an isomorphism over the smooth locus.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the products $Z_i$, bundles $\\bar E_i$, and scalings above, there is a constant $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $i$, a projective resolution $\\pi_i:\\widetilde Z_i\\to Z_i$ over $k_i$ carries a nonzero section defined over $k_i$, $$s_i\\in H^0(\\widetilde Z_i,\\pi_i^*E_i^{\\otimes n_i})\n \\quad\\text{for some integer }n_i>0$$ whose adelic supremum norms satisfy $$\\label{eq:small-section-norms}\n \\|s_i\\|_{v,\\mathrm{sup}}\\le\n \\begin{cases}\n \\exp(-cn_iM_i^2),&v\\mid\\infty,\\\\\n 1,&v\\nmid\\infty.\n \\end{cases}$$ The resolution may be chosen to be an isomorphism over the smooth locus.",
      "explanation": "Arithmetic positivity yields nonzero sections with exponential archimedean norm decay over the original bounded-degree fields.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-index",
        "exact_quote": "Fix projective embeddings of abelian varieties $A_\\ell$ by symmetric very ample bundles $L_\\ell$, a symmetric very ample canonically metrized bundle $\\bar L$ on $\\mathcal A=\\prod_\\ell A_\\ell$, and integral bases of the homomorphism groups between the fixed varieties. Let $F_i:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal A$ be homomorphisms. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$y_{i\\ell}$ is a smooth point of an irreducible $Z_{i\\ell}\\subset A_\\ell$, its projective height is $O(H_{i\\ell})$, and its conormal space is spanned by equations of bounded degree and coefficient height $O(H_{i\\ell})$, with $H_{i\\ell}\\ge1$;*\n\n2.  *$M_i\\longrightarrow\\infty$, the positive integers $b_{i\\ell}$ satisfy $b_{i\\ell}\\ll M_i$ and $b_{i\\ell}^2H_{i\\ell}\\asymp M_i^2$, and the coefficients of $F_i|_{A_\\ell}$ in the fixed bases are $O(b_{i\\ell})$;*\n\n\n3.  *for $\\bar E_i=F_i^*\\bar L$ one has $h_{\\bar E_i}(y_i)=o(M_i^2)$, and a nonzero section $s_i$ of $E_i^{n_i}$ on a resolution of $Z_i=\\prod_\\ell Z_{i\\ell}$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:index-small-section}\n     \\|s_i\\|_{v,\\sup}\\le\n     \\begin{cases}\n     e^{-c n_iM_i^2},&v\\mid\\infty,\\\\\n     1,&v\\nmid\\infty,\n     \\end{cases}$$ for a fixed $c>0$, over a number field containing its data.*\n\n*Assume that the resolution is an isomorphism over the smooth locus. Then, for some $\\eta>0$ independent of $i$, $\\operatorname{ind}_{n_i,b_i}(s_i;y_i)\\ge\\eta$ for all sufficiently large $i$. No bound on the degree of the field of $y_i$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-index",
          "exact_quote": "Fix projective embeddings of abelian varieties $A_\\ell$ by symmetric very ample bundles $L_\\ell$, a symmetric very ample canonically metrized bundle $\\bar L$ on $\\mathcal A=\\prod_\\ell A_\\ell$, and integral bases of the homomorphism groups between the fixed varieties. Let $F_i:\\mathcal A\\to\\mathcal A$ be homomorphisms. Suppose that:*\n\n1.  *$y_{i\\ell}$ is a smooth point of an irreducible $Z_{i\\ell}\\subset A_\\ell$, its projective height is $O(H_{i\\ell})$, and its conormal space is spanned by equations of bounded degree and coefficient height $O(H_{i\\ell})$, with $H_{i\\ell}\\ge1$;*\n\n2.  *$M_i\\longrightarrow\\infty$, the positive integers $b_{i\\ell}$ satisfy $b_{i\\ell}\\ll M_i$ and $b_{i\\ell}^2H_{i\\ell}\\asymp M_i^2$, and the coefficients of $F_i|_{A_\\ell}$ in the fixed bases are $O(b_{i\\ell})$;*\n\n\n3.  *for $\\bar E_i=F_i^*\\bar L$ one has $h_{\\bar E_i}(y_i)=o(M_i^2)$, and a nonzero section $s_i$ of $E_i^{n_i}$ on a resolution of $Z_i=\\prod_\\ell Z_{i\\ell}$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:index-small-section}\n     \\|s_i\\|_{v,\\sup}\\le\n     \\begin{cases}\n     e^{-c n_iM_i^2},&v\\mid\\infty,\\\\\n     1,&v\\nmid\\infty,\n     \\end{cases}$$ for a fixed $c>0$, over a number field containing its data.*\n\n*Assume that the resolution is an isomorphism over the smooth locus. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **special subvariety** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *special subvariety* of $A_{\bar K}$ is a torsion coset $B+\tau$, where $B$ is an abelian subvariety and $\tau$ is a torsion point.
- **special closure** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a subvariety $Z$, write $\langle Z\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$ for the smallest special subvariety containing $Z$.
- **atypical component** ([source](#eq:atypical)): Let $X\subseteq A_{\bar K}$ and put $S=\langle X\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$. An irreducible component $Y$ of $X\cap T$, for a special $T\subseteq S$, is *atypical for $X$ in $S$* if $$\label{eq:atypical} \dim Y>\dim X+\dim T-\dim S.$$
- **height space and height norm** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For an abelian variety $G$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, choose a symmetric ample line bundle and write $$\mathcal H(G)=G(\overline{\mathbb Q})\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R, \qquad \\|x\\|^2=\widehat h(x).$$
- **tangent integral** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Let $Z=\prod_{\ell\in I}Z_\ell$ be a product of subvarieties of $\prod_\ell A_\ell$, and let $g$ be a complex linear map from its ambient Lie algebra to a Hermitian vector space with positive $(1,1)$-form $\omega$. Define $$\label{eq:tangent-integral} P_Z(g)=\int_Z(g^*\omega)^{\dim Z},$$ where the pullback is regarded as an invariant form on the ambient abelian variety.
- **geodesic closure** ([source](#pm-h-13)): For a subvariety $D$ of a complex abelian variety, its *geodesic closure* $\langle D\rangle_{\mathrm{geo}}$ is the smallest coset of an abelian subvariety containing $D$.
- **geodesic defect** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a subvariety $D$ of an abelian variety, put $$\delta_{\mathrm{geo}}(D) =\dim\langle D\rangle_{\mathrm{geo}}-\dim D.$$
- **geodesic optimality** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A subvariety $D\subseteq Y$ is *geodesic-optimal in $Y$* if every proper enlargement $D\subsetneq E\subseteq Y$ has $\delta_{\mathrm{geo}}(E)>\delta_{\mathrm{geo}}(D)$.
- **weighted vanishing index** ([source](#sec:index)): For a smooth point $y=(y_\ell)_\ell$ of a product $Z=\prod_\ell Z_\ell$, choose regular parameters $T_\ell=(T_{\ell,1},\ldots,T_{\ell,d_\ell})$ on each factor and a local frame $e$ of a line bundle $E$. If a nonzero section of $E^n$ has expansion $s=e^n\sum_\alpha a_\alpha T^\alpha$, define $$\label{eq:weighted-index} \operatorname{ind}_{n,b}(s;y) =\min_{a_\alpha\ne0} \sum_\ell\frac{\|\alpha_\ell\|}{n b_\ell^2}.$$
- **special defect** ([source](#sec:finiteness)): For an irreducible subvariety $Z$ of an abelian variety, its *special defect* is $$\delta(Z)=\dim\langle Z\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}-\dim Z.$$
- **optimal subvariety** ([source](#sec:finiteness)): An irreducible $Z\subseteq X$ is *optimal in $X$* if every irreducible $U$ with $Z\subsetneq U\subseteq X$ satisfies $\delta(U)>\delta(Z)$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lemma-height-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-height-decomposition): Maximal quotient extraction and complete reducibility. -> Separated height scales
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lemma-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-projection): Project a minimal ambient counterexample and compare local fiber dimensions with lost rank. -> Projection exclusion
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-ax-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ax-product): Apply Ax’s theorem and degenerate the subgroup direction by factor scalings. -> Product tangent obstruction
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-directions): Enlarge to geodesic-optimal subvarieties of fixed parents. -> Finite parent directions
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-tangent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tangent): Pass to fixed subgroup directions and use closed rank inequalities; compare consecutive-difference deficits. -> Tangent positivity
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lemma-intersection-lower-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-intersection-lower-bound): Integer finite differences and real quantifier elimination bound a positive minimum on a rational box. -> Degree-dependent intersection lower bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-all-high) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-all-high): Fix the height-inequality constant before rational approximation and balance divergent scales with dyadic multipliers. -> All-high exclusion
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-minimal-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-minimal-products): Minimize admissible factor dimensions and exclude proper bounded-degree cuts. -> Smooth marked products with fixed low factor
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lemma-fixed-low-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-fixed-low-point): Remove a fixed low point using several separated high subsequences. -> Positive-dimensional fixed low factor
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-arithmetic-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arithmetic-positive): Compare limiting curvature densities and apply uniform adelic metric variation on the fixed low factor. -> Positive normalized arithmetic intersection
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lemma-small-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-small-sections): Subtract a constant adelic bundle and apply arithmetic Siu. -> Small sections over the defining fields
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-prop-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-index): Combine Cauchy coefficient estimates with the product formula and uniform local radii. -> Uniform positive weighted index
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-prop-product-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-product-cut): Take finite projections and field norms; apply the product theorem and conjugate-counting bound. -> Contradiction completing low-factor exclusion
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-prop-sequence-implies-nondensity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sequence-implies-nondensity): Retain slower coordinate histories and pass subgroup projectors to a real limit. -> Universal non-density
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-prop-optimal-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-optimal-finite): Apply the universal optimal-subvariety reduction and check all subgroup hypotheses. -> Optimal-subvariety finiteness
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-maximal-optimal-2): Translate to the special closure and use optimality of maximal atypical subvarieties. -> Main finiteness theorem
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-rank-translates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-maximal-optimal-3): Embed the translated intersection in an auxiliary abelian variety using finite-rank integral relations. -> Finite-rank translated non-density and curve finiteness

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: Maximal atypical subvarieties form a finite exceptional list when atypicality is measured in the smallest containing torsion coset.
- **I2**: The high-codimension special-locus intersection is not Zariski dense under the no-proper-torsion-coset hypothesis.
- **I3**: Finite-rank translated intersections are not Zariski dense under the stronger exclusion of every translate of a proper algebraic subgroup.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Use the finiteness theorem as a terminating enumeration algorithm with a uniform stopping bound. -> Qualitative finiteness supplies no effective stopping bound for the exceptional list.
- **A2**: Treat non-density as finiteness of every translated intersection. -> A proper closed exceptional subset may have positive dimension.
- **A3**: Apply the translated-intersection corollary using only exclusion of proper torsion cosets. -> The translated statement requires exclusion of arbitrary proper algebraic-subgroup translates.
- **A4**: Replace tangent positivity by a uniform numerical lower bound over every subproduct. -> Positivity alone does not provide that numerical bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves qualitative finiteness and non-density for intersections with varying torsion cosets. It explicitly gives no effective or uniform bound for the number of maximal atypical subvarieties. Its sequence arguments pass to subsequences and use asymptotic height and measure comparisons; these do not supply a finite enumeration contract for the exceptional subvarieties. Curve finiteness likewise does not provide an effective enumeration procedure.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (nonconstructive): Finiteness of maximal atypical subvarieties supplies no effective or uniform bound on their number. Blocked use: ['Using the finiteness conclusion as a bounded enumeration algorithm.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The exceptional list may be empty. Blocked use: ['Assuming that every subvariety has an atypical component.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The non-density conclusion requires the irreducible subvariety to lie in no proper torsion coset. Blocked use: ['Applying this conclusion to a subvariety contained in a proper torsion coset without a reduction of the ambient variety.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The finite-rank translated conclusion requires exclusion of every translate of a proper algebraic subgroup. Blocked use: ['Replacing this transversality requirement by exclusion only of proper torsion cosets.']
- **L5** (counterexample_scope): The translated-intersection deduction concerns algebraic points in abelian varieties and does not establish the general semiabelian complex formulation. Blocked use: ['Applying this deduction to arbitrary complex translating points or general semiabelian varieties.']
- **L6** (edge_case): In higher dimensions translated intersections are shown to be non-dense; their exceptional subset can have positive dimension. Blocked use: ['Promoting higher-dimensional non-density to finiteness of intersection points.']
- **L7** (approximation): Tangent positivity alone does not give a uniform positive numerical bound over all subproducts. Blocked use: ['Using tangent positivity as a fixed numerical margin over all subproducts.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Bounded degrees of auxiliary varieties and their defining fields do not impose bounded degrees on marked points. Blocked use: ['Restricting the argument to marked points of bounded degree.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The local index conclusion assumes a resolution that is an isomorphism over the smooth locus, together with the stated smoothness, scale and small-section hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Applying the local index conclusion at arbitrary singular points without its hypotheses.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): The small-section exponent need not be bounded. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform bound on the power needed to obtain a small section.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the abelian Zilber–Pink conjecture over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$. Every irreducible subvariety of an abelian variety has only finitely many maximal atypical subvarieties, where atypicality is measured inside its smallest containing torsion coset.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A dimension count predicts how a subvariety of an abelian variety meets a fixed torsion coset. The abelian Zilber–Pink conjecture asks for a finiteness statement when the intersection is larger than this prediction, even though the torsion coset is allowed to vary. We prove the conjecture over number fields in arbitrary dimension.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The finiteness statement

Fix a number field $K$, an algebraic closure $\bar K$, and an abelian variety $A/K$. All subvarieties below are closed and irreducible over $\bar K$. A *special subvariety* of $A_{\bar K}$ is a torsion coset $B+\tau$, where $B$ is an abelian subvariety and $\tau$ is a torsion point. For a subvariety $Z$, write $\langle Z\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$ for the smallest special subvariety containing $Z$.

<a id="eq:closure-monotonicity"></a>
This smallest subvariety exists: choose a special subvariety of least dimension containing $Z$, and intersect it with any other such subvariety. Each irreducible component of a nonempty intersection of torsion cosets is a torsion coset. The component containing $Z$ has the same dimension as the chosen one, and hence equals it. This also proves uniqueness and the implication $$\label{eq:closure-monotonicity}
 Z\subseteq Z'\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \langle Z\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}\subseteq
 \langle Z'\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}.$$ Here the assertion about intersections follows by intersecting the underlying algebraic subgroups: a nonempty intersection is a coset of their intersection, torsion points lift through surjective homomorphisms of abelian varieties, and every component of an algebraic subgroup of an abelian variety contains a torsion point.

<a id="eq:atypical"></a>
Let $X\subseteq A_{\bar K}$ and put $S=\langle X\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$. An irreducible component $Y$ of $X\cap T$, for a special $T\subseteq S$, is *atypical for $X$ in $S$* if $$\label{eq:atypical}
 \dim Y>\dim X+\dim T-\dim S.$$ Maximality of an atypical subvariety always means maximality for inclusion among the atypical subvarieties of $X$ in $S$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every number field $K$, every abelian variety $A/K$, and every irreducible subvariety $X\subseteq A_{\bar K}$, there are only finitely many maximal atypical subvarieties of $X$ in $S=\langle X\rangle_{\mathrm{sp}}$. Equivalently, there are finitely many proper atypical subvarieties $Y_1,\ldots,Y_m\subsetneq X$ such that every atypical subvariety is contained in one of the $Y_j$.*

</div>

The list can be empty. No bound on the degrees or heights of the intersection points is assumed, and no simplicity, endomorphism, or complex multiplication hypothesis is imposed on $A$. The theorem concerns torsion cosets in abelian varieties; it gives no effective or uniform bound for the number of maximal atypical subvarieties.

For a torsion coset $S$ and an integer $r\ge0$, set $$S^{[r]}=\bigcup_{\substack{T\subseteq S\ \mathrm{special}\\
                         \mathop{\mathrm{codim}}_S T\ge r}}T.$$ We first prove the following non-density theorem, with universal quantifiers on the ambient abelian variety and its subvarieties.

<a id="stmt-thm-nondensity"></a>
<div id="thm:nondensity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *Let $A$ be an abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and let $X\subseteq A$ be an irreducible subvariety contained in no proper torsion coset. Then $X\cap A^{[\dim X+1]}$ is not Zariski dense in $X$.*

</div>

The passage from this assertion to Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> uses the optimal-subvariety reduction of Barroero and Dill. Section <a href="#sec:finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finiteness">9</a> checks its universal hypotheses and gives the final inclusion argument. In particular, the proof does not stop with non-density for one fixed $X$.

The same universal non-density theorem also gives a consequence for translations by finite-rank subgroups. For point sets $E,\Gamma\subseteq A(\overline{\mathbb Q})$, write $E+\Gamma=\{e+\gamma:e\in E,\ \gamma\in\Gamma\}$.

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-rank-translates"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-rank-translates" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Finite-rank translated intersections). *Let $A$ be an abelian variety over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, let $\Gamma\subseteq A(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ be a subgroup satisfying $$\dim_{\mathbb Q}\bigl(\Gamma\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q\bigr)<\infty,$$ and let $X\subseteq A$ be an irreducible closed subvariety of dimension $d$ contained in no translate of a proper algebraic subgroup of $A$. Then $$X(\overline{\mathbb Q})\cap\bigl(A^{[d+1]}(\overline{\mathbb Q})+\Gamma\bigr)$$ is not Zariski dense in $X$. If $d=1$, this intersection is finite.*

</div>

This is the abelian algebraic-point case of Pink’s Conjecture 5.2 (Pink 2005) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Pink2005](#ref-Pink2005)]. The group $\Gamma$ need not be finitely generated: torsion subgroups and division hulls of finitely generated subgroups are included. Section <a href="#sec:finite-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite-rank">10</a> applies Pink’s reduction (Pink 2005, Theorem 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Pink2005](#ref-Pink2005)] and records where the stronger no-proper-translate hypothesis is used.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context

Zilber formulated the unlikely-intersection principle for semiabelian varieties (Zilber 2002) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Zilber2002](#ref-Zilber2002)]. Pink’s semiabelian formulation (Pink 2005, Conjecture 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Pink2005](#ref-Pink2005)] specializes to the high-codimension non-density statement considered here.

The torsion-point case belongs to the Manin–Mumford theorem of Raynaud (Raynaud 1983, 327, Théorème) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Raynaud1983Torsion](#ref-Raynaud1983Torsion)]: a subvariety with dense torsion points is a torsion coset. Unlikely intersections allow the torsion points to be replaced by torsion cosets of varying positive dimensions. Even when these cosets have large codimension, their intersections can contain positive-dimensional components; the maximality condition in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> accounts for this possibility.

For curves in powers of a CM elliptic curve, Viada proved codimension-two finiteness under the hypothesis that the curve lies in no translate of a proper algebraic subgroup (Viada 2003, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Viada2003](#ref-Viada2003)]. Rémond and Viada removed this transversality restriction, retaining the necessary exclusion of proper algebraic subgroups (Rémond and Viada 2003, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[RemondViada2003](#ref-RemondViada2003)]. Habegger and Pila proved finiteness for curves in arbitrary abelian varieties over number fields (Habegger and Pila 2016, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HabeggerPila2016](#ref-HabeggerPila2016)] <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BarroeroKuhneSchmidt2023](#ref-BarroeroKuhneSchmidt2023)]. Barroero, Kühne and Schmidt subsequently established the corresponding curve theorem for general semiabelian varieties over number fields (Barroero, Kühne, and Schmidt 2023, Theorem 1.1). More recently, Dogra and Pandit obtained bounded-degree results for smooth curves whose Jacobians surject onto the ambient abelian variety. Together with established height bounds, these give finiteness. Their local theorem gives explicit reduction bounds under good-reduction and genus hypotheses, and cardinality bounds under further restrictions on the simple isogeny factors (Dogra and Pandit 2026, Theorem 1 and Corollary 1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DograPandit2026](#ref-DograPandit2026)].

Higher-dimensional non-density was known under additional geometric or height restrictions. For positive-dimensional $X$, consider the condition $$\dim\pi(X)=\min\{\dim X,\dim(A/B)\}
 \qquad\text{for every quotient }\pi:A\longrightarrow A/B.$$ In the CM case, Rémond proved finite-rank translated non-density under this condition (Rémond 2009, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Remond2009](#ref-Remond2009)]; Habegger independently proved the untranslated case (Habegger 2009, Corollary 2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Habegger2009](#ref-Habegger2009)]. Viada obtained a non-CM elliptic-power case (Viada 2009, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Viada2009](#ref-Viada2009)]. Habegger and Pila established non-density under the same quotient condition for arbitrary abelian varieties (Habegger and Pila 2016, Theorem 1.3(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[HabeggerPila2016](#ref-HabeggerPila2016)]. Without that condition, their Theorem 1.3(i) gives non-density at every fixed height bound when $X$ is contained in no proper torsion coset.

There were also higher-dimensional finiteness results for maximal atypical subvarieties. Habegger and Pila’s results imply finiteness of those of codimension one in $X$ (Habegger and Pila 2016, Theorem 9.14(i)) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HabeggerPila2016](#ref-HabeggerPila2016)]. For codimension-two subvarieties of powers of an elliptic curve contained in no proper torsion coset, maximal-atypical finiteness was proved in the CM case by Checcoli, Veneziano and Viada (Checcoli, Veneziano, and Viada 2014, Corollary 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CheccoliVenezianoViada2014](#ref-CheccoliVenezianoViada2014)] and without the CM restriction by Hubschmid and Viada (Hubschmid and Viada 2019, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[HubschmidViada2019](#ref-HubschmidViada2019)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> treats arbitrary abelian varieties and all codimensions, without the additional quotient-dimension or height restrictions.

The geometric inputs are Ax’s analytic-subgroup theorem (Ax 1972) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Ax1972](#ref-Ax1972)], in the precise analytic-subset form stated in (Habegger and Pila 2016, Theorem 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[HabeggerPila2016](#ref-HabeggerPila2016)], and the finiteness of directions of geodesic-optimal subvarieties of a fixed parent (Habegger and Pila 2016, Proposition 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[HabeggerPila2016](#ref-HabeggerPila2016)]. Habegger and Pila explicitly attribute the latter finiteness to earlier results of Rémond (Rémond 2009, Lemma 2.6 and Proposition 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Remond2009](#ref-Remond2009)].

The height argument follows Vojta’s curve method (Vojta 1991) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Vojta1991](#ref-Vojta1991)], Faltings’ extension to abelian varieties (Faltings 1991) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Faltings1991](#ref-Faltings1991)], and Rémond’s generalized inequality (Rémond 2005) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Remond2005Vojta](#ref-Remond2005Vojta)]. We use Dill’s version with separate height thresholds for the factors; his introduction credits Ange with the preceding extension to different projective varieties (Dill 2020) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Dill2020](#ref-Dill2020)]. The bounded-scale argument adapts the two-measure comparison in the Bogomolov proofs of Ullmo and Zhang (Ullmo 1998; S.-W. Zhang 1998a) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Ullmo1998](#ref-Ullmo1998), [Zhang1998](#ref-Zhang1998)], based on Szpiro–Ullmo–Zhang equidistribution (Szpiro, Ullmo, and Zhang 1997) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[SzpiroUllmoZhang1997](#ref-SzpiroUllmoZhang1997)]. Zhang’s account (S.-W. Zhang 1998b, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Zhang1998ICM](#ref-Zhang1998ICM)] explicitly traces the consecutive-difference map to Faltings. The metric-variation calculation uses Yuan’s arithmetic bigness method (Yuan 2008) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Yuan2008](#ref-Yuan2008)], in the nef adelic form of Yuan and Zhang (Yuan and Zhang 2025) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[YuanZhang2021](#ref-YuanZhang2021)]. The local weighted-index argument follows Faltings’ method, and the final cut uses his product theorem in Evertse’s explicit form (Evertse 1995) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Evertse1995Product](#ref-Evertse1995Product)].

The estimates proved here keep these inputs uniform across several height scales and across auxiliary varieties with bounded fields of definition. The argument for the bounded scale compares measures only on the fixed low factor; it does not invoke an equidistribution theorem for a fixed variety as a substitute for uniformity.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy and organization

The proof studies a hypothetical Zariski-generic sequence of high-codimension intersection points. The square roots of their Néron–Tate heights need not be comparable. Repeatedly taking a quotient of largest dimension on which the height is smaller separates the ambient variety, up to isogeny, into at most one bounded level and finitely many successively larger unbounded levels. Normalizing each level separately makes the point vectors bounded. Their algebraic subgroup relations then converge to a real-endomorphism projector that sends the normalized vectors to zero in the limit.

The auxiliary sequence theorem in Section <a href="#sec:sequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sequences">2</a> excludes the high-codimension limit just obtained. Its geometric input is uniform tangent positivity, obtained in Section <a href="#sec:tangent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tangent">3</a> from Ax’s theorem and the finiteness of geodesic-optimal directions in a fixed parent variety. Projection to smaller abelian varieties handles every possible failure of this positivity.

There are two arithmetic parts. When every height level is unbounded, Section <a href="#sec:high" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:high">4</a> applies Dill’s generalized Vojta–Rémond inequality. The relevant multiplicative constant is independent of the accuracy of a rational approximation to the limiting projector. This permits the approximation error to be made small before taking points of sufficiently large height.

A bounded level requires a different argument. Section <a href="#sec:minimal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:minimal">5</a> chooses auxiliary products of minimal dimension whose degrees and fields of definition are bounded. These bounds concern the varieties, while the marked points may have arbitrarily large degree. A zero-dimensional bounded factor reduces to the unbounded case. For a positive-dimensional bounded factor, Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">6</a> proves a uniform arithmetic intersection lower bound. The proof compares two limiting measures on that fixed factor: one from a product map and one from a consecutive difference map. The first has positive density at a suitable diagonal point; the second has zero density there. A variational argument on the changing varieties converts this disagreement into arithmetic positivity.

Small sections supplied by that positivity have a uniformly positive weighted vanishing index at the marked points (Section <a href="#sec:index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:index">7</a>). The product theorem then yields a proper factor through a marked point. A multiplicity calculation bounds both its degree and the number of its Galois conjugates (Section <a href="#sec:product-cut" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:product-cut">8</a>). This contradicts minimality and completes the sequence theorem.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

We identify $\bar K$ with $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ and fix an embedding $\overline{\mathbb Q}\hookrightarrow
\mathbb C$. A coset without the adjective special may be translated by an arbitrary point. Its direction is the abelian subvariety being translated. All logarithmic heights and arithmetic intersection numbers are absolute, normalized by the degree of the field of definition. Constants in asymptotic estimates may depend on fixed varieties, embeddings and polarizations, but not on the sequence index unless explicitly stated. The notation $u_i\asymp v_i$ means that both ratios are bounded above by positive constants.

<a id="sec:sequences"></a>
### Height scales and an auxiliary sequence theorem

The proof separates coordinates whose heights grow at different rates. We first explain the linear algebra that permits real homomorphisms to act on normalized points. We then state the sequence theorem and show that it implies Theorem <a href="#thm:nondensity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:nondensity">2</a>. Its proof occupies the subsequent sections.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Real homomorphisms and height norms

For an abelian variety $G$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, choose a symmetric ample line bundle and write $$\mathcal H(G)=G(\overline{\mathbb Q})\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R,
 \qquad \|x\|^2=\widehat h(x).$$ The canonical height pairing extends to a positive-definite real quadratic form on $\mathcal H(G)$. Indeed, every finite collection of points is defined over one number field, and the assertion on its real span is the positive definiteness of the Néron–Tate pairing on the Mordell–Weil group modulo torsion. We use the same notation for a point and its image in $\mathcal H(G)$. In particular, torsion points have image zero. Polarizations also give Hermitian forms on the complex Lie algebras $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$. On products we choose product polarizations and product norms. We use the standard complex uniformization, complete reducibility, finite-rank homomorphism groups and polarization adjoints recalled in (Milne 2008, chap. I, Sections 2, 10 and 14) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Milne2008AV](#ref-Milne2008AV)]. Canonical metrics and heights are taken with the multiplication normalization of (S. Zhang 1995, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Zhang1995](#ref-Zhang1995)].

<a id="stmt-lemma-real-calculus"></a>
<div id="lemma:real-calculus" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Real homomorphism calculus). *Let $G,G'$ be abelian varieties over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ with the preceding choices. Every element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G,G')_{\mathbb R}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G,G')\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb R$ acts on both $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$ and $\mathcal H(G)$. The Lie action is faithful. Convergence in this finite-dimensional real homomorphism space implies operator-norm convergence on the height spaces.*

*The polarization adjoint of a real homomorphism is a real homomorphism. Its kernel and image in the Lie algebras have orthogonal projectors in the corresponding real endomorphism algebras, and it has a generalized inverse in the opposite real homomorphism space. In particular, if $f$ is fixed and $\|f(x_i)\|\to0$, then $$\|P_{(\ker f)^\perp}(x_i)\|\longrightarrow0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lemma-real-calculus"><summary>Proof of stmt-lemma-real-calculus</summary>

*Proof.* Fix integral homomorphisms $f_1,\ldots,f_b$ forming a real basis of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(G,G')_{\mathbb R}$. For each $j$, ample domination of $f_j^*L'$ by a power of $L$ gives $\widehat h_{L'}(f_j(x))\le C_j\widehat h_L(x)$ for all algebraic points $x$. The same inequality holds on $\mathcal H(G)$, by its quadratic-form interpretation on every finite-dimensional span. Hence $$\left\|\sum_j t_j f_j(x)\right\|
 \le \left(\sum_j |t_j|\sqrt{C_j}\right)\|x\|.$$ The constants are independent of the fields of definition of the points. This proves the assertion about operator norms.

The underlying real Lie representation identifies with the real extension of the representation on rational homology. It is faithful, also after scalar extension from $\mathbb Q$ to $\mathbb R$. The polarization adjoint belongs to rational homomorphisms, and therefore extends to real homomorphisms. For a real homomorphism $f$, the operator $f^*f$ is nonnegative and self-adjoint on $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$. Choose a real polynomial $p$ that is zero at zero and equals $1/t$ at every positive eigenvalue $t$ of $f^*f$. Then $$f^\dagger=p(f^*f)f^*$$ is a real homomorphism and $f^\dagger f=P_{(\ker f)^\perp}$, $ff^\dagger=P_{\operatorname{im}f}$. Faithfulness makes these identities identities of real homomorphisms, so they also hold on the height spaces. The last assertion follows by applying the fixed bounded operator $f^\dagger$. ◻

</details>

In particular, the image of any real homomorphism into $G$ is the image of a real idempotent endomorphism of $G$. Images, inverse images, and intersections of finitely many such Lie subspaces can therefore be handled by real homomorphisms. These subspaces need not be Lie algebras of abelian subvarieties.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The sequence theorem

Local fiber dimension at a point always means the maximum of the dimensions of the irreducible components of the fiber through that point. This convention matters when a marked point is singular.

The fiber bounds in the sequence theorem record the dimension contributed by each height level. Let $W_1,W_2$ be abelian varieties. Consider an irreducible $X\subseteq W_1\times W_2$, points $(p_{i1},p_{i2})\in X$, and scales $H_{i\ell}\ge1$ with $\|p_{i\ell}\|=O(\sqrt{H_{i\ell}})$ and $H_{i1}/H_{i2}\to0$. Retain the first coordinate when forming the second ambient factor: $$\begin{array}{lll}
 A_1=W_1,&Y_1=\operatorname{pr}_1(X),&m_1=\dim Y_1,\\
 A_2=W_1\times W_2,&Y_2=X,&m_2=\dim X-\dim Y_1.
 \end{array}$$ The distinguished subgroups are $W_1\subseteq A_1$ and $\{0\}\times W_2\subseteq A_2$. The integers $m_1,m_2$ are the generic dimensions of the corresponding fibers, and $m_1+m_2=\dim X$. The copied coordinate $p_{i1}/\sqrt{H_{i2}}$ tends to zero, so it disappears after normalization at the second scale. In the reduction below, genericity of the sequence ensures that the marked points eventually have these fiber dimensions. The following definition allows arbitrary pairs $W_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$, accommodating both this construction with any number of levels and the quotients used in Section <a href="#sec:tangent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tangent">3</a>.

<a id="stmt-def-sequence-data"></a>
<div id="def:sequence-data" class="definition">

<a id="eq:sequence-fibers"></a>
**Definition 5** (Sequence data). Let $I$ be the ordered list $1,\ldots,s$, possibly preceded by $0$. The list is nonempty; if $0$ occurs, $s$ may be zero. For each $\ell\in I$, fix an abelian variety $A_\ell$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, a nonzero abelian subvariety $W_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$, an irreducible subvariety $Y_\ell\subseteq A_\ell$, and an integer $m_\ell\ge0$. Put $$\mathcal A=\prod_{\ell\in I}A_\ell,
 \qquad W=\prod_{\ell\in I}W_\ell,
 \qquad D_\ell=A_\ell/W_\ell.$$ For positive integers $i$, let $y_{i\ell}\in Y_\ell(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ and $H_{i\ell}\ge1$ satisfy $$\label{eq:sequence-fibers}
 \dim_{y_{i\ell}}\bigl(Y_\ell\cap(y_{i\ell}+W_\ell)\bigr)
 \le m_\ell,
 \qquad \|y_{i\ell}\|=O(\sqrt{H_{i\ell}}).$$ For consecutive indices $\ell,\ell'$ in $I$, require $H_{i\ell}/H_{i\ell'}\to0$.

<a id="eq:sequence-nondegenerate"></a>
The factors $\ell\ge1$ are called high factors. They satisfy $H_{i\ell}\to\infty$, and, for every fixed homomorphism $g:A_\ell\to G$ to an abelian variety whose restriction to $W_\ell$ is nonzero, $$\label{eq:sequence-nondegenerate}
 \liminf_{i\to\infty}
 \frac{\|g(y_{i\ell})\|}{\sqrt{H_{i\ell}}}>0.$$ If the index $0$ occurs, it is called the low factor and satisfies $W_0=A_0$, $H_{i0}=1$. Its points eventually avoid each fixed proper torsion coset of $A_0$.

</div>

The homomorphism in <a href="#eq:sequence-nondegenerate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sequence-nondegenerate">[eq:sequence-nondegenerate]</a> is integral; the condition is equivalently unchanged by allowing rational homomorphisms. Its positive lower bound may depend on $g$. No bound on the degrees of the marked points is assumed.

<a id="stmt-thm-sequence"></a>
<div id="thm:sequence" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:forbidden-sequence"></a>
**Theorem 6** (Auxiliary sequence theorem). *For sequence data as in Definition <a href="#def:sequence-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:sequence-data">5</a>, let $V\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(W)$ be the image of an idempotent in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(\mathcal A)_{\mathbb R}$.
